1476.TT 儒鴻 
 評價低緣、高殖利率，投資價值浮現

[法說會更新]   
   公司簡介 
儒鴻成立於 1977 年，生產各式針織布及成衣，從染整、織布、定型、成衣，擁有垂
直整合生產供應鏈，並切入機能性服飾市場，2025 年針織布營收 127.7 億元，YoY-
2.3%，營收占比 33.61%，成衣營收 252.2 億元，YoY+6.2%，比重占 66.39%。 
投資評等/目標價 重點摘要 
買進 416 
【前次投資建議】買進，396 
【大盤指數/股價】42712 / 348.5

➢ 3Q26 獲利略低於投研部預估 
公司 3Q26 營收 105.72 億元，YoY+9.7%，營收略低於投研部預估之
106.8 億元，主要係因成衣營收低於投研部預期所致，毛利率 31.18%，
優於投研部預期，主要受惠高毛利率新產品及新客戶占比提升，且匯率
亦有所助益(約影響毛利率 0.62 個百分點)，費用率因業績獎金提列較高
而推升至 10.49%，營益率 20.69%，低於投研部預估之 21.24%，稅後
EPS 6.36 元，低於投研部預估之 6.57 元。 
➢ 4Q26 展望正向，略為上修 4Q26 獲利預估 
10/2026 營收 34 億元，YoY+10.7%，符合投研部預期，本次法說會管
理層表示對 4Q26 營運維持 TITAS 2026 釋出之看法不變，4Q26 營收可
望為年內高峰，上修 4Q26 營收預估至 105.99 億元，YoY+14.4%，毛
利率因原料成本負面影響趨緩，公司高毛利率新產品及新客戶占比持續
提升，毛利率同步上修至 31.05%，YoY+0.86ppt.，預估稅後淨利 17.25
億元，YoY+9%，稅後 EPS 上修至 6.29 元。目前訂單能見度維持 6 個
月，展望正向，期中選舉民主黨勝選預期對關稅壁壘政策可望有所趨緩，
且美伊戰爭亦可望有所緩解，市場不確定因素減少亦有利於整體經濟消
費動能，印尼廠一期、二期目前維持穩定獲利且產能滿載，投研部上修
2026 年營收預估至 408.77 億元，YoY+7.6%，毛利率因新產品、新客
戶營收占比持續提升，略為上修至 31.09%，YoY+2.45ppt.，稅後 EPS
上修至 24.99 元。  
➢ 2027 年營運成長可期，評價處歷史區間低緣且殖利率逾 5%  
公司目前始承接 04/2027 訂單，觀察美國總體經濟動能強勁，消費者支
出可望維持韌性，惟考量公司現有廠區產能趨於滿載，目前僅以原有產
能小規模擴充為主，投研部以過去幾年公司中性成長均值預估 2027 年
營收 431.95 億元，YoY+5.7%，毛利率因 4Q27 印尼新廠投產而略為承
壓，預估 2027 年毛利率 30.62%，YoY-0.47ppt.，營益率預估 20.74%，
YoY+0.01ppt.，稅後 EPS 預估由 26.19 元略為上修至 26.31 元，投研部
以現金股利配發率 74.63%預估，現金股利殖利率可望達 5.35%，且依
未來四季合計稅後 EPS 估算目前評價仍處過往歷史區間低緣位置，長期
投資價值浮現，投研部維持評價 PER 16X 並以未來四季稅後 EPS 估算目
標價，目標價由 396 元上修至 416 元(4Q26~3Q27F PE 16X)，維持買
進投資建議。

個股報告

儒鴻為國內成衣廠龍頭 
儒鴻成立於 1977 年，生產各式針織布及成衣，從染整、織布、定型、成衣，擁有垂
直整合生產供應鏈，並切入機能性服飾市場，2025 年針織布營收 127.7 億元，YoY-
2.3%，營收占比 33.61%，成衣營收 252.2 億元，YoY+6.2%，比重占 66.39%；營收
區域別來看，2025 年美洲營收占比 50.87%，YoY+4.5%，亞洲營收占比 35.19%，
YoY+2.6%，歐洲營收占比 10.29%，YoY+10.2%，中東營收占比 2.49%，YoY-17.5%，
紐澳營收占比 0.85%。YoY+16.2%，非洲營收占比 0.31%。YoY-64.7%。 
3Q26 獲利略低於投研部預估  
公司 3Q26 營收 105.72 億元，QoQ+4.8%，YoY+9.7%，營收略低於投研部預估之
106.8 億元，主要係因成衣營收低於投研部預期所致，針織布營收則優於投研部預期，
毛利率 31.18%，QoQ+1.17ppt.，YoY+2.52ppt.，優於投研部預估之 30.76%，主要
受惠高毛利率新產品及新客戶占比提升，且匯率亦有所助益(約影響毛利率 0.62 個百
分點)，費用率因業績獎金提列較高而推升至 10.49%，高於投研部預估之 9.53%，營
益率 20.69%，QoQ+1.51ppt.，YoY+1.59ppt.，低於投研部預估之 21.24%，業外則
有匯兌收入認列 1,327 萬元，稅後淨利 17.44 億元，QoQ+12.3%，YoY+5.6%，稅
後 EPS 6.36 元，低於投研部預估之 6.57 元。

個股報告 
4Q26 展望正向，略為上修 4Q26 獲利預估 
10/2026 營收 34 億元，MoM-3.1%，YoY+10.7%，YTD+5.9%，占投研部原預估
4Q26 營收達成率 33.4%，符合投研部預期，公司過去 10 月單月營收占 4Q26 營收
占比區間介於 29.5%~34.2%，本次法說會管理層表示對 4Q26 營運維持台北紡織展
(TITAS 2026)釋出之看法不變，以訂單來看，若出貨船期順利，則 4Q26 營收可望為
年內高 峰， 基此 ，投研 部 上修 4Q26 營收 預估 至 105.99 億 元，QoQ+0.3%，
YoY+14.4%，毛利率因原料成本負面影響趨緩，公司高毛利率新產品及新客戶占比持
續提升，毛利率亦同步上修至 31.05%，QoQ-0.14ppt.，YoY+0.86ppt.，並同步上修
營益率至 20.33%，QoQ-0.36ppt.，YoY+1.17ppt.，預估稅後淨利 17.25 億元，QoQ-
1.1%，YoY+9%，稅後 EPS 由 5.84 元略為上修至 6.29 元。 
展望 2026 年，訂單能見度維持 2 個季度，訂單展望維持正向，公司表示目前訂單尚
未因美國期中選舉結果出爐後而有所變動，然期中選舉民主黨勝選預期對關稅壁壘政
策可望有所趨緩，且美伊戰爭亦可望有所緩解，市場不確定因素減少亦有利於整體經
濟消費動能，印尼廠一期、二期目前維持穩定獲利且產能滿載，目前協力廠外發比重
維持 27%~28%，因 4Q26 營收及獲利略為上修，投研部亦同步上修 2026 年營收預
估至 408.77 億元，YoY+7.6%，毛利率部份，因高毛利率新產品、新客戶營收占比可
望 持 續 提 升 ， 且 匯 率 環 境 相 較 於 2025 年 亦 較 為 友 善 ， 略 為 上 修 31.09% ，
YoY+2.45ppt.，營益率則由 20.62%上修至 20.73%，YoY+2.06ppt.，並預估稅後淨
利 68.56 億元，YoY+24.3%，稅後 EPS 由 24.77 元上修至 24.99 元。 
圖 1：公司歷年月營收趨勢               單位：千元

資料來源：國票投顧彙製 
2027 年營運可望維持成長，目前評價處歷史區間低緣且殖利率逾 5% 
展望 2027 年，目前已開始承接 04/2027 訂單，雖訂單能見度未達 2027 年底，且客
戶端 2027 年預示單仍洽談中，然觀察美國就業環境仍穩健，初領及續領失業救濟金
人數仍處歷史低檔，總體經濟動能強勁，消費者支出可望維持韌性，且 2026 年營運
受戰爭、通膨影響較為劇烈，研判 2027 年可望較為淡化，惟考量公司現有廠區產能
趨於滿載，印尼三期實際貢獻營收時點落於 4Q27~1Q28，目前僅以原有產能小規模
擴充產能為主，若市況優於預期則以外發協力廠因應，2027 年外發比重大致上介於
27%~35%區間，基此，投研部以過去幾年公司中性成長均值預估 2027 年營收 431.95
億元，YoY+5.7%，主要為產品報價提升之貢獻，並預估毛利率因 4Q27 印尼新廠投

研 究 報 告                
中砂(1560.TT) 
產業：電機機械 
上修資本支出用於擴產隱含展望正向，看好毛利率延續成⻑趨勢 
 投資評價與建議 
3Q26毛利率表現優於預期，且公司上修資本支出用於擴產，隱含主要業務展
望正向，加上公司積極優化產品結構隱含明年營收、獲利均具上修空間；我們
給予40倍PE，維持對公司業務之正向看法，並看好毛利率維持成⻑動能。 
 重點評論分析 
4Q26三大業務營收、獲利持續改善：觀察ABU業務中，預期高階砂輪與ABF鑽
⽯機密模具需求持續成⻑，DBU業務出貨動能則維持強勁，加上公司規劃針對
SBU部分品項漲價約10%，將帶動營收、毛利率改善。我們預估本季營收有望
季增5.5%至28.4億元，毛利率44.5%，稅後EPS 4.37元。 
上修今年資本支出預算用於擴產與新品開發，隱含營運前景正向：基於DBU、
SBU客戶需求強勁，及公司持續優化產品組合，公司上修今年資本支出預算至
22.6億元(原16~17億元)，除了先前已規畫SBU 3Q27月產能至43萬片(已宣
布)，本次則上修今明年底鑽⽯碟月產能分別至8、11萬顆(原7萬顆以上/9.5萬
顆)，此外將購買土地廠房用於開發先進封裝用之減薄鑽⽯砂輪新品，以及後續
鑽⽯碟擴產需求。而在2027年展望方面，我們仍維持2027年三大業務均正向
成⻑之看法，其中DBU、SBU成⻑動能最為強勁，另ABU業務中，公司布局之
先進封裝用之減薄砂輪有望於2H27貢獻營收。預估2027年營收年增25.7%至
129.7億元，毛利率46%，稅後EPS 21.7元。 
 投資風險 
DBU擴產進度不如預期。

### Page 2

2

| 營運概況 
3Q26毛利率表現優於預期：3Q26營收季增8.1%至26.9億元，毛利率42.2%，稅後EPS 3.88元，其中毛利率受惠
於：(1)台灣工具機客戶需求回溫、ABF鑽⽯機密模具用量增加，帶動ABU高階產品比重提升，挹注該業務毛利率
由2Q26的超過25%提升至3Q26的35%；(2)針對DBU大客戶之2/1.6奈米、先進封裝鑽⽯碟用量持續增加，使營收
規模擴大，我們推估毛利率由2Q26的50~55%提升至55%以上。 
4Q26三大業務營收、獲利持續改善：觀察：(1)ABU業務中，預期高階砂輪與ABF鑽⽯機密模具需求持續成⻑，至
於泰國、日本子公司出貨動能則持平看待，預估營收微幅季增，毛利率則有望持續向上；(2)DBU業務中，出貨動
能則維持強勁，因台系大客戶先進製程、封裝需求維持強勁，另美系記憶體、IDM客戶需求呈現逐季成⻑，加上
成熟製程廠商產能利用率持續提升，預估營收仍有雙位數季增；(3)SBU業務中，12吋月產能維持40萬片滿載，但
公司計畫針對部分品項價格調漲約10%，加上持續優化產品組合，將帶動營收、毛利率改善。預估本季營收有望
季增5.5%至28.4億元，毛利率44.5%，稅後EPS 4.37元。 
上修今年資本支出預算用於擴產與新品開發，隱含營運前景正向：基於DBU、SBU客戶需求強勁，及公司持續優
化產品組合，公司上修今年資本支出預算至22.6億元(原16~17億元)，除了先前已規畫SBU 3Q27月產能至43萬片
(已宣布)，本次則上修今明年底鑽⽯碟月產能分別至8、11萬顆(原7萬顆以上/9.5萬顆)，此外將購買土地廠房用於
開發先進封裝用之減薄鑽⽯砂輪新品，以及後續鑽⽯碟擴產需求。而在2027年展望方面，我們仍維持2027年三大
業務均正向成⻑之看法，動能包含：(1)預估DBU主要客戶A16/2、3奈米明年底月產能將由今年底的9~10、15~17
分別提升至14~16、19~20萬片，尤其先進製程CMP道數、規格相較於成熟製程提升，將帶動鑽⽯碟用量、平均
單價增加，另美系IDM合作順利(目前公司於18A市占約50%，公司持續推進14A之合作)，加上成熟製程、記憶體
客戶擴產挹注；(2)SBU除了有明年額外3萬片之產能挹注，公司針對現有產能也將持續透過產品組合優化等方式改
善獲利，且看好2H27美系記憶體特殊晶圓用量將比1H27成⻑；(3)ABU方面，則基於台灣ABF廠商持續擴產，公
司布局之新品先進封裝減薄砂輪有望在2H27於新廠貢獻營收，加上公司將中低毛利率產品搬至泰國、日本子公司
生產以此優化成本結構。我們預估2027年營收年增25.7%至129.7億元，毛利率46%，稅後EPS 21.7元。

Document 034: 2026-11-11_［みずほ経済インサイト］堅調なAI需要で大幅増益が視野_1ea94428

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-11-11_［みずほ経済インサイト］堅調なAI需要で大幅増益が視野_1ea94428.pdf
Report source: Mizuho
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

1

堅調なAI 需要で大幅増益が視野 
投資増につながるかが焦点に

○ 製造業の業績見通しは大幅に改善している。輸出向けを中心とするAI関連財の需要拡大が背景。
2026年度は好業績が期待され、二桁増益が視野に入る 
○ AI関連財の増産を背景に、国内製造業の設備不足感は2025年後半以降強まっている。足元では関
連業種の機械受注が増加しており、AI需要拡大が国内設備投資に波及する兆しがみられる 
○ 設備投資は緩やかな拡大が息長く続く見通し。ただし、米国でデータセンター建設規制が実施さ
れれば、輸出・業績・設備投資の好循環が逆回転に転じるリスクも

１．企業景況感は AI 関連の製造業を中心に改善 
AIの学習・利用に必要なデータセンター投資や半導体需要が世界的に堅調であることを背景に、日
本の製造業の景況感は幅広く改善している。日銀短観（2026年9月調査）では、大企業・製造業の業況
判断DIが6月調査から改善した（図表1）。業種別では、半導体や電子部品などを含む電気機械や、半
導体製造装置を含む生産用機械の改善が際立つ。そのほか、窯業・土石製品、非鉄金属、半導体材料・
素材を扱う化学などでも改善がみられた。ロイター短観（10月調査）でも、「半導体関連市場の需要
が強い」との声が散見され、AI関連需要の堅調さがうかがえる。 
AI関連財の輸出が拡大し、足元で企業の業績見通しは大きく改善している。企業業績の改善を国内
の需要拡大につなげるうえで、次の焦点となるのが設備投資である。企業業績の改善に比べて、投資

などの内需への恩恵はまだ明確ではなく、今後設備投資の増加として発現する可能性が期待される。 
２．AI 関連財輸出は好調。2026 年度の製造業は二桁増益が視野に 
世界的なAIサービスの普及に伴い、データセンターや関連機器への投資が拡大している。大規模な
データセンターを保有・運営し、クラウドサービスやAI向けの計算基盤を提供するIT企業、とりわけ
ハイパースケーラーと呼ばれる大規模事業者を中心に、高水準の設備投資を継続する見通しが示され
ていることを踏まえると、AI関連投資の高い伸びは当面持続する見込みである。こうしたなか、モデ
ルの演算を担う処理装置のほか、大量のデータを蓄える記憶装置、サーバー同士をつなぐ通信ケーブ
ル、電力を安定供給する電源装置などといった財において投資需要が高まっている。日本企業が供給
する財についても、電子部品・機器への需要が増加しているほか、AI向け半導体の増産投資は日本の
半導体製造・検査装置や材料への需要を押し上げている。 
図表2は、輸出全体から、WTOの定義に従ってAI関連財を抽出したものだ。AI関連財輸出は2025年終
盤以降加速しており、8月時点では前年比＋29.1％と高い伸びになった。主因は半導体・電子部品で、
旺盛な需要のもとで半導体価格が大幅に上昇し、輸出金額を押し上げている。また、半導体製造・検
査機器や、データセンターに組み込まれる機械・部材、半導体の製造に用いられる原材料・素材など、
幅広いAI関連財が輸出増に寄与している。 
輸出額の高い伸びを受け、製造業では業績見通しが改善している。日銀短観（9月調査）の2026年度
経常利益計画（全規模・製造業）は、6月調査時点から大幅に上方修正され、前年度比＋11.6％の増益
が見込まれる（図表3）。特に電気機械では、上期の堅調な輸出実績を反映して、経常利益計画が大き
く上方修正された。このほか、業況判断DIの改善が目立った業種でも同様の傾向がみられた。堅調な
AI関連需要が下期も続けば、製造業では大幅増益が期待される。

３．AI 関連業種の投資需要は増勢加速の兆しも 
今後の実質GDPへの影響を見通すうえでの焦点は、企業収益の拡大が設備投資を中心とした内需の押
し上げにどの程度繋がるかだ。この点、AI需要の追い風を受け好業績が見込まれることを踏まえれば、
設備投資の伸びはやや物足りなさが否めない。日銀短観（9月調査）によると、製造業の2026年度設備
投資計画は直近5年間では最も低い伸びに留まっている（図表4）。建材や生産設備の価格上昇、金利
上昇に伴う資金調達コストの増加が投資負担となり、好業績のもとでも計画の積み増しを抑えている
可能性がある。 
もっとも、設備投資需要は計画対比で伸び余地がある。AI関連財を中心に増産が進むなか、能力増
強投資の必要性が高まっているためだ。AI関連財の生産は2025年秋以降、はっきりとした増加傾向が
続いている（図表5）。生産・営業用設備判断DI（全規模・製造業）も2025年後半から低下しており、
製造業の設備不足感は強まっている（図表6）。 
実際、AI需要の拡大が今後の設備投資を押し上げる兆しもみられる。図表7は、設備投資の先行指標
とされる機械受注（国内需要向け）を需要者別に分解し、価格変動の影響を取り除いたものだ。これ
をみると2025年夏場以降、情報通信機械、電気機械、はん用・生産用機械といった業種からの受注が
急増していることがわかる。結果として、半導体製造装置をはじめとするAI・データセンター関連品
目では受注残高が積み上がっている（図表8）。 
ただし、機械受注が設備投資として顕在化するまでにはタイムラグを伴うとみられる。そもそも、
受注残高が積み上がっている背景には、旺盛な需要だけでなく、供給面の制約による影響があると考
えられる1。このため、設備投資の増加ペースは緩やかなものにとどまる公算が大きいとみているが、
その分、今後は息の長い投資拡大局面が続くだろう。

図表 5 鉱工業生産 図表6 生産・営業用設備判断DI

### Page 4

４．今後は政府の投資支援策も民間投資を後押し。米国の規制強化リスクには要注意 
以上のように、先行きの設備投資は、設備不足感の強まるAI関連産業の投資需要に支えられ、緩や
かに増勢を強めると見込まれる。 
また今後は、政府の政策支援が、投資コストの負担軽減を通じて、設備投資を後押しするとみられ
る。7月末に始動した政府の「大胆な投資促進税制」では、一定の要件を満たす国内投資について、即
時償却や税額控除の適用を認める減税措置が設けられた。さらに、2027年度予算の編成では、特別予
算枠として『「強く豊かな日本」投資枠』を設け、民間投資を促す支援策が検討されている。具体的
な支援策が整えば、複数年度にわたる投資計画の実行を支えよう。 
なお、11月3日に投開票された米国中間選挙の結果を受け、米国におけるデータセンター投資が下振
れるリスクにも触れておきたい。同選挙では、データセンター建設計画の多いテキサス州やジョージ
ア州の州知事選などにおいて、建設の一時停止を掲げる民主党候補が勝利した。米国では、データセ
ンター建設規制に関して州政府や地方自治体が大きな権限を持つ。 
実際、市場ではAI関連企業の業績・投資下振れが意識され、グローバルにハイテク銘柄の株価が急
落した。仮に今後、米国でデータセンター建設が規制されれば、日本でも半導体・電子部品や製造装
置などの輸出が減少し、企業業績が下押しされる可能性がある。その際、AI関連財が減産に転じて設
備不足感が弱まれば、日本国内における能力増強投資の需要も後退しかねない。新知事の任期開始お
よび新議会の招集は2027年1月だが、当面の米国政治動静については注意深くみていく必要があろう。

1 なお、供給制約に起因して、資本財の輸入浸透度（国内供給に占める輸入品の割合）が一層上昇する可能性には留意が必要で
ある。今後、国内の供給余力が限られるなかで投資需要が一層輸入品に向かえば、国産資本財への需要が減退し、投資需要増に
よる GDP の押し上げ効果が限られるリスクがある。

Inventec (2356.TW)
10 November 2026 Citi Research

Bull/Bear: Inventec (2356.TW)

### Page 6

Inventec (2356.TW)
10 November 2026 Citi Research
6
Inventec
Company description
Established in 1975 and listed in 1996, Taiwan-based Inventec is a key ODM
of NBs, servers, smartphones, and wearable/IoT devices to global customers.
Major shareholders include the founder Mr. Yeh Kuo-I, whose family has (both
directly and indirectly) a c35% stake.
Investment strategy
We rate Inventec shares as Buy. We expect stable demand in NB and server to
help support near-term earnings, while product diversification within smart

devices to areas such as IoT and medical space should help improve L-T
margins. We expect the company's solid cash yield to serve as a downside
cushion.
Valuation
Our target price of NT$65 for Inventec shares is based on 17x 2027 EPS, which
is slightly above the mid-point of its historical trading range, reflecting the
company's product diversification and stable demand outlook led by servers
and commercial NBs, as well as increasing AI server exposure that could

potentially drive margin expansion and better earnings momentum.
Risks
Key downside risks that could impede the stock from reaching our target price
include: 1) Slower-than-expected shipment growth/weaker-than-expected
sell-through in HomePods and AirPods could lead to downside in our sales
forecasts; 2) If competitors adopt aggressive pricing strategies to gain market
shares, our ASP and margin assumptions could face downside risk; 3) Lower-
than-expected server margins driven by rising exposure to Chinese customers

could impact our earnings forecasts negatively; 4) Deterioration in NB
demand and investment in solar would hurt our forecasts; and 5) Stronger-
than-expected Taiwan dollar could lead to FX loss and downside to our
current forecasts.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

GS Factor Profile  ______________________________

GCE’s 3Q26 core business (OPI) was 6%/1% below GSe/BBG 
consensus due mainly to (1) key AI customer’s product transition 
period (revenue in 3Q was 4/3% lower than GSe/BBG consensus), 
and (2) the increasing investment in R&D to improve its 
production capability on AI HDI and N+M MLB products), despite 
the much stronger than expected GM (0.8/1.8ppt higher than 
GSe/BBG consensus). For the net income in 3Q26, it was 5% below 
our bullish estimate, resulting from the weaker than expected 
operating profit, but it was 3% higher than BBG consensus, thanks to 
the lower than expected tax rate resulting from the increasing profit 
contribution from the low tax rate Thailand plant (tax rate was only 
at 25% in 3Q26 vs. BBG consensus at ~30%).  
For 4Q26, we expect the company’s revenue to grow by 7% QoQ, 
due to ongoing key AI customers’ product transition, and the fewer 
working days due to the holiday season. We expect GCE’s GM will 
further expand to 39.2% (up 0.5ppt QoQ or 5.5ppt YoY) thanks to 
the better product mix (high-end networking demand should ramp 
up more significantly from 4Q26, per GSe), despite the increasing 
cost from high-end CCL (see here).  
Investment view - GCE will continue to deliver a better margin 
profile with expanding market share in AI ASIC market 
We continue to expect GCE will hold the major share for Trainium 3 
(70%+ from 3Q26), and see an even better market share in the next 
generation Trainium 4 products (as key competitors are all unable to 
support high-end AI HDI products) after the company ramps up its 
AI HDI production yield rate. We also expect the company to start 
supplying the new TPU v8 PCB products, which should meaningfully 
improve the company’s market share in coming quarters (from <5% 
in 2026 to 20-30%+ in 2027), suggesting an even better GM outlook 
in the foreseeable future.  
On the other hand, the company will only expand its capacity

### Page 3

volume by ~20% in 2027, without considering the product mix improvement/ASP hike 
(we believe after considering the better ASP for next-gen AI products, the company’s 
capacity value should go up by 35%+ YoY in 2027), while we believe the AI PCB 
volume/area demand should at least increase by 50% YoY, implying a potential market 
share loss in coming quarters. We note Greater China peers are also expanding capacity 
in SEA regions to try to penetrate into GCE’s key AI ASIC markets, implying increasing 
competition in coming quarters, which could lead to less favorable ASP in the long term, 
despite no significant impact for now. However, considering GCE’s best in class AI PCB 
production technology and its proactive investment in the high-end PCB R&D for more 
than 10 years, as well as the company’s first mover advantage in the SEA PCB market, we 
expect GCE will continue to have opportunities to expand its business in the most 
high-end PCB market, suggesting a good GM outlook in the long term.  
Maintain Buy on GCE, with a new 12-m TP of NT$1,660 (from NT$1,800).

Earning and TP revision

Earnings revision 
We revise down our 2026/27/28E net income estimate by 9%/6%/5% to factor in the 
potential slower pricing hike outlook in the PCB industry, considering the increasing 
competition in the AI PCB industry (cut 2026/27/28E revenue estimate by 7/6/5%). 
Moreover, we believe GCE will need to invest more in R&D projects in the coming 
quarters to improve its HDI production capability, and expect the N+M PCB R&D 
investment will also drive OPEX in coming years, considering the increasing adoption 
rate from 1H27 (revise up 2027 OPEX to sales ratio by 0.3ppt).

### Page 4

TP revision 
Maintain Buy with a new 12-m TP of NT$1,660 (from NT$1,800), based on 15x P/E 
(down from 20x previously, still in line with AI PCB peers’ average valuation); we also 
rollover our valuation base from 2027E to 2H27-1H28E, and factor in our earnings 
revision.

Investment thesis and Valuation & Risks

GCE is a key cloud (server+switch) PCB supplier globally with 20%+ market share in 
2022. We expect cloud PCB market demand to grow at a 15% 2024-26E CAGR (far 
stronger than the overall RPCB+HDI market demand growth rate at 5% 2024-26E 
CAGR). We believe GCE’s proactive capacity expansion plan (33% in 2026) and high 
annualized ROI (300%+) will continue to drive the company’s revenue/margin level in the 
coming quarters/years. Additionally, we believe GCE can continue to see strong demand 
from the increasing content per server/switch, as we expect both markets to see a 
20-50%+ layer count increase after the generation upgrade (upgrade to 400G/Eagle 
Stream). We are constructive on GCE’s long-term growth opportunity given its solid 
position in the rapidly growing cloud PCB market, and we are Buy rated on the stock. 
GCE is trading higher than its historical P/E average level driven by strong AI server 
demand, but lower than the TW AI server players’ average. 
Price Target Risks and Methodology 
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$1,660 is based on a 15x 2H27-1H28E P/E 
(in line with AI PCB peers average valuation).  
Key downside risks: (1) weaker-than-expected AI server market demand; (2) delays in 
the company’s Nvidia OAM/UBB product qualification, (3) weaker-than-expected HDI 
demand, and (4) higher-than-expected ramp up cost for new capacity

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

### Page 1

ab 11 November 2026
Global Research
First Read
Nan Ya Plastics
Glass fabric capacity expansion to strengthen
vertical integration
What's new
NYP announced plans to invest NT$11.5bn to add 102mn meters of glass fabric capacity
(+17% vs. existing 590mn meters capacity) in Zhangbin, Taiwan, by end-2029.
According to management, the expansion aims to meet growing demand from AI
servers, networking equipment and increasingly complex PCBs, with 60–70% of the

additional capacity allocated for internal use. Capacity will flexibly serve conventional E-
glass and AI-grade specialty glass fabric, with specialty products targeted to reach ~40%
of glass fabric revenue in 2-3 years. NYP is also expanding upstream yarn production and
adding over 500 weaving looms to support its planned glass fabric capacity expansion
by end-2029.
Implications
NYP: Glass fabric expansion to strengthen vertical integration; 1.5–2.0% EM OP

upside from 2030
We view the expansion positively, as glass fabric remains the most supply-constrained
product across NYP's CCL value chain, with the highest OPM of c40% in 3Q26 for glass
fabric. We expect further margin improvement in 4Q26, driven by continued price hikes
and a higher specialty glass fabric mix amid strong AI-related demand. Over the longer
term, the expansion should strengthen NYP's vertical integration and secure upstream
glass fabric supply for downstream CCL production. Assuming full utilization and

current ASP and margins, we estimate the 17% capacity expansion could lift EM OP by
1.5–2.0% from 2030 onwards, with further upside potential from specialty product mix
upgrades.
E-glass fabric: limited impact on external supply
NYP's planned capacity expansion of 102mn meters per year is equivalent to ~2% of
total industry capacity according to our estimates. We expect limited impact on external
E-glass supply, as NYP plans to use 60-70% of output internally, leaving only ~30-40mn

meters annually for external sales before accounting for specialty fabric allocation.
AI-grade specialty fabric: supply constraints likely to persist
We expect supply tightness to persist amid specialty yarn constraints and qualified fabric
production ramp-up. NYP's undisclosed capacity mix further limits visibility on
incremental AI-grade fabric supply.
Valuation: Maintain Buy and PT at NT$390/share
NYP is trading at 10x 27E P/E and 3.5x 27E P/B on our estimates.  Our 26/27E dividend

yields are 3.8%/6.3% at the current share price. We expect limited near-term valuation
impact from the glass fabric expansion, given its expected completion by end-2029.

Company Description
Established in 1958 and listed on the TAIEX in 1967, Nan Ya Plastics one of the core
companies of Taiwan's Formosa Group. Its businesses span electronic materials, chemicals,
plastics processing and polyester. NYP is one of the largest copper clad laminates (CCL)

producers, with a vertically integrated CCL value chain, supplying CCL to its 61%-owned
subsidiary Nan Ya PCB and other printed circuit board (PCB) makers. It owns 29% of Nan Ya
Tech, the only Taiwan-based dynamic random access memory (DRAM) brand. It also owns
23% of Formosa Petrochemical.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
Nan Ya Plastics:
We base our price target for NYP on a SOTP valuation.
Key upside risks include: 1) stronger CCL pricing; 2) faster high-end CCL mix upgrades; and 3)

a faster petchem margin recovery.
Key downside risks are: 1) a CCL pricing reversal on faster supply additions or weaker
demand; 2) slower AI qualification/ramp of the company's CCL business; and 3) further
weakening chemicals/polyester businesses.
Nan Ya Printed Circuit Board:
Our price target for Nan Ya Printed Circuit Board is based on P/E multiple methodology.
Upside risks include faster-than-expected ramp-up of networking/switches, stronger-than-

expected pricing increase, better-than-estimated consumer demand, and continued tightness
in BT substrates.
Downside risks include lower-than-expected ramp-up of networking/switch projects, lower-
than-expected global PC/server demand, oversupply from capacity expansions, raw material
cost increases, intensified competition, and a lack of spec upgrades and content increase.
As for industry risks, substrate applications are dominated by PCs, but servers, artificial

intelligence and advanced computing are driving demand growth. We believe key risks to the
industry include slower-than-expected growth in servers, networking, and high performance
computing, ASP pressure, margin erosion, and lack of spec upgrades. We believe the flexible
circuit board and high-density interconnect board segments are more vulnerable to
fluctuations in the consumer product cycle and have lower visibility.
Nanya Technology:
Nanya Tech is a pure-play DRAM manufacturer based in Taiwan mainly focused on legacy DDR

products for customers across various end applications. We think downside risks to our thesis
could include a potential slowdown in end demand and hence the conventional memory
cycle as well as Nanya's legacy-centric product portfolio. We value Nanya Tech shares using a
target NTM P/BV multiple based on long-term average ROE and cost of equity.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

個股報告 
群聯(8299) 
AI 需求未減，營收規模持續擴大 
投資評等 目標價 
買進 $2150 
前次投資建議：2026.08.14： 
「買進」，目標價: $4000

報告日期：2026.11.09 研 究 員：王穎

摘 要 及 調 整 原 因 
➢ 看好 AI及企業級相關產品需求維持強勁，且 AI產品組合佔比將進一步擴大，帶動營收規模
擴大，維持「買進」投資評等，然低價庫存利益將逐季下滑導致毛利率下降，因此下修目
標價至 2150元(4 x 27(F)PER)，潛在上漲空間 43.3%。

基本資料 結論與建議 
2026/11/6 收盤價： 1,500.00 ➢ 26Q3 營收 860.76 億元，QoQ+26.8%，YoY+374.6%，
毛利率 59.9%，費用 184.89 億元，QoQ+5.25 億元，業
外獲利 30.77億元，稅後淨利 305.46億元，EPS 138元。
受惠產品組合轉向企業級及 AI 應用，使營收規模擴大，
然手機、PC 等消費性需求受到 NAND 漲價下出貨量下
滑，但群聯本身 controller 出貨量及營收仍提升，主要因
市占率提升，同時低價庫存利益將減少導致毛利率下滑。 
➢ 展望 26Q4 預期 NAND Falsh 報價持續提升且企業級及
AI相關之產品需求維持強勁，預期 AI Ecosystem module
將持續提升，預期營收續創新高，然庫存成本提高使毛利
率下滑。因此兆豐預估營收 991.6 億元，QoQ+15.2%，
YoY+334.9%，毛利率 56.4%，稅後淨利 308.22 億元，
EPS為 139.3元。 
➢ 展望 2026~2027 年，預期至 2027 仍呈現供不應求之態
勢，公司坐擁大量庫存，且群聯產品組合轉向 AI 
Ecosystem module 有助於有利營收規模持續放大，然隨
著庫存成本持續提升，預期毛利率將下滑。因此兆豐預估
營收 2940.9/4442.2 億元，YoY+304.7/51.1%，毛利率
60.1/51.7% ，稅後淨利 1027.6/1187.7 億元，
YoY+1075.8/15.6%，EPS 為 464.7/536.9元。 
➢ 群聯目前位於 2.8 X 27(F)PER)，維持「買進」投資評等，
下修目標價至 2150 元(4 x 27(F)PER)，潛在上漲空間
43.3%。

群聯為企業級 SSD領導廠商 
群聯電子股份有限公司成立於 2000年 11月 8 日，為國內 NAND FLASH控
制 IC 廠，主要業務為 NAND FLASH 控制 IC及周邊系統產品的研發設計製
造及銷售。公司已經成為 USB隨身碟、SD記憶卡、eMMC、UFS、PATA、
SATA 與 PCIe 固態磁碟等控制晶片領域的領頭廠。26Q3 產品組合 AI 
Ecosystem Module 57%、 Embedded ODM Module 24%、 Industrial 
Module9%、Controller 5%、Retail Module2%、Others 3%。 
         圖一、群聯 26Q3產品營收占比 
             群聯產品以 AI Ecosystem 相關產品為主

- 3 - 
個股報告 
26Q3產品組合轉佳帶動營收會模擴大 
26Q3 營收 860.76 億元，QoQ+26.8%，YoY+374.6%，毛利率 59.9%，費
用 184.89 億元，QoQ+5.25 億元，業外獲利 30.77 億元，稅後淨利 305.46
億元，EPS 138元。受惠產品組合轉向企業級及 AI應用，使營收規模擴大，
然手機、PC 等消費性需求受到 NAND 漲價下出貨量下滑，但群聯本身
controller 出貨量及營收仍提升，主要因市占率提升，預期待消費性及工業市
場歷經庫存調整後，有望逐步恢復。同時低價庫存利益將減少導致毛利率下
滑。26Q3 庫存金額 1117.7 億元，較 26Q2 增加 199.8 億元，存貨週轉天數
由 297天下降至 274天，存貨主要配置於企業級、AI應用及 boot drive 等應
用，以因應後續客戶需求。

26Q4預期營收規模擴大獲利持續創高 
展望 26Q4預期 NAND Falsh報價持續提升，於 main drive持續取得不同客
戶 design win，main drive對於 NAND容量需求更大，於 26Q3取得多項新
專案，預期 26Q4~27Q1將持續增加。同時 PCIe Gen6企業級 SSD預期 2026
年 11 月底開始提供樣品，與 CPU 業者共同發布相關 solution，主要鎖定 AI
推論相關應用，因此企業級及 AI相關之產品需求將於 2027年維持強勁，預
期 AI Ecosystem module 將持續提升，預期營收續創新高，然庫存成本提高
使毛利率下滑。因此兆豐預估營收 991.6億元，QoQ+15.2%，YoY+334.9%，
毛利率 56.4%，稅後淨利 308.22億元，EPS 為 139.3元。

- 4 - 
個股報告 
股利配發創下新高 
26H1 EPS 達 187.4元，董事會決議通過上半年度盈餘分配現金股利案，每
股擬配發 60 元現金股利，創下歷史新高，配發率約 32%，半年殖利率約
2.63%。若 26H2獲利及現金流更佳，2027年 1月可再評估提高配發比例，
全年殖利率目標有望達 5%。 
圖三、NAND歷年供需比

資料來源：兆豐投顧整理

2026 NAND Flash 供需缺口逐步擴大 
NAND FLASH市場呈現明顯的週期性循環，每約 3.5～4年進入一個完整供
需週期，2026 NAND Flash 呈現供不應求，且下半年供需缺口擴大，有利報
價穩定甚至持續上漲。同時 Nvidia ICMS (Inference Context Memory 
Storage) 的快取層級架構，用於解決大型語言模型推論時的記憶體瓶頸，推
升 NAND需求，提升 Token吞吐量 5 倍、推論延遲顯著下降、能效優化 5 倍。
預期 2027年用於 Nvidia KV Cache 之 NAND 需求將達總需求約 10%。進一
步造成供需缺口擴大。

- 5 - 
個股報告 
預期 AI 佔比提升帶動營收規模放大 
2026~2027 年，預期至 2027 仍呈現供不應求之態勢，公司坐擁大量庫存，
main drive、PCIe Gen6等產品預期至 2027年都將持續成長使群聯產品組合
轉向 AI Ecosystem module 有助於有利營收規模持續放大，另外在消費性市
場持續耕耘，取得更高手機、PC市占率，Client SSD方面，2026年已取得
6 個 PCIe Gen5 TLC 及 QlC controller 專案，未來將協助原廠開發相容
aiDAPTIV之 Client SSD方案，結合 AI PC於記憶體擴充需求，擴大 PC市
場影響力，因此出貨量將持續增加，待消費性庫存歷經調整後，出貨量將進
一步提升，然隨著庫存成本持續提升，預期毛利率將下滑。因此兆豐預估營
收 2940.9/4442.2 億元，YoY+304.7/51.1%，毛利率 60.1/51.7%，稅後淨利
1027.6/1187.7 億元，YoY+1075.8/15.6%，EPS為 464.7/536.9元。

看好 AI 相關產品組合佔比提升，維持「買進」投資評等 
群聯目前位於 2.8 X 27(F)PER)，看好 AI及企業級相關產品需求維持強勁，
且 AI產品組合佔比將進一步擴大，維持「買進」投資評等，然低價庫存利益
將逐季下滑導致毛利率下降，因此下修目標價至 2150元(4 x 27(F)PER)，潛
在上漲空間 43.3%。

Document 244: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점불확실성보다 성장_3801b631

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점불확실성보다 성장_3801b631.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
3분기 실적 컨센서스 상회 
삼성바이오로직스의 2026년 3분기 매출액은 1조 4,903억 원(+18.5% YoY), 
영업이익은 6,401억 원(+1.0% YoY, 영업이익률 43.0%)으로 시장 컨센서스를 각각 
10%, 5% 상회했다. 1~4공장의 풀가동이 이어진 가운데 5공장의 인증용 생산배치 
물량과 미국 락빌 공장 매출(약 1,000억 원)이 처음으로 반영되며 외형 성장을 
견인했다. 매출원가율은 50.4%로 전년 동기(41.4%), 전분기(47.0%) 대비 
상승했는데, 락빌 공장과 5공장의 초기 물량 반영에 따른 것으로 판단한다. 향후 
물량 상승에 따라 매출원가율은 감소할 것으로 예상한다. 그럼에도 EBITDA 
마진율은 50.4%로 높은 수준을 유지하며 7,515억 원을 달성했다. 순이익은 원/달러 
환율 하락에 따른 외화환산손실이 반영되며 3,911억 원을 기록했다.  
환율 하락에도 가이던스 상향하며 성장 궤도 유지 
2026년 연간 매출액은 5조 6,133억 원(+23.2% YoY), 영업이익은 2조 4,605억 
원(+18.9% YoY)으로 전망한다. 최근 원/달러 환율 하락에도 이번 실적발표에서 
상향 제시한 연간 매출 성장률 20% 이상은 충분히 달성 가능할 것으로 판단한다. 
4분기에는 락빌 공장의 매출 약 1,000억 원과 5공장 가동 확대에 더해, 5월 
파업으로 이연된 물량의 매출 인식이 성장에 기여할 전망이다.  
투자의견 매수 및 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
비만 치료제 CDMO 진출을 위한 폴리펩타이드 그룹 인수는 공개매수 응모율 
87.88%로 최소 조건을 충족해 11월 말 완료를 앞두고 있어 신규 성장 동력은 
확보된 것으로 판단한다. 노사 리스크도 법원의 쟁의행위 제한 확대에 이어 노조가 
2차 총파업을 잠정 연기하며 크게 완화됐다. 미국 관세 불확실성으로 부진했던 신규 
수주 모멘텀은 올해 연말부터 내년에 걸쳐 강화될 것으로 전망하며, 불확실성 
해소와 성장 기대감을 반영할 시점이라 판단한다.

Compliance Notice 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

Document 264: AI boom or doom_ what will superintelligence feel like and when will we get there_ (2026-10-12)

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI boom or doom_ what will superintelligence feel like and when will we get there_ (2026-10-12).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-10-12
Pages: 20
Status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

AI boom or doom: what will 
superintelligence feel like and  
when will we get there?

1 
Authors 
Adrian Cox 
Research Analyst

Azeem Azhar, Exponential View 
Expert Collaborator

October 12, 2026 
The recent panic about rogue AI agents collaborating in swarms to take over the 
internet and threaten human existence has, more than occasionally, lacked 
nuance. But it does reflect how artificial intelligence is progressing in creative 
and surprising ways, and at a pace that seems uncontrollable. 
For some in the industry, it is evidence that the technology is following a path of 
accelerating self-improvement that will lead to artificial general intelligence (AGI) 
and, perhaps, to utopia or catastrophe. 
Former Google CEO Eric Schmidt has sketched out a “San Francisco 
Consensus” based on a belief in scaling laws, with ever larger AI models 
becoming an input into their own improvement; superintelligence by the end of 
the decade; and transformative AI systems outperforming humans on many 
tasks. This loop would lead to exponential growth in scientific advancement, 
financial returns and human progress. And crucially, AGI, and then 
superintelligence, would merely be milestones in a process, far from its climax. 
If that were the case, superintelligence could make much of the cognitive work 
that dominates the world economy much cheaper and change the ways in which 
firms compete. But its economic effects will depend on how businesses and 
customers respond. 
In this paper, we analyse what the San Francisco Consensus means for investors 
and enterprises in the years to come and what could accelerate – or slow down – 
its timeline of AGI by 2030. 
1. What would superintelligence look and feel like? 
2. Can we get there theoretically? 
3. Can we get there practically? 
4. What happens if we don’t get there? The base case 
The question is urgent. 
And it is complicated by reports of AI models autonomously accessing the 
internet and hacking other companies; a public backlash over the energy-
intensive data centres springing up as part of a historic $1 trillion a year 
investment boom; and dire warnings of threats to life if superintelligent AI runs 
roughshod over its human creators to achieve its own goals.

### Page 2

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 2

1. What would superintelligence look and feel like? 
1.1 What are AGI and superintelligence? 
Artificial general intelligence (AGI) is a simple milestone scribbled in pencil: an AI 
system that has surpassed human capabilities. Superintelligence is the 
continuum beyond that point. It is not merely systems that exceed the best 
human performance across one cognitive task or another, but systems that 
exceed human potential across every cognitive task. 
The San Francisco Consensus takes this further: the intelligence must be able to 
produce more intelligence. The key to getting there is so-called recursive self-
improvement, where the AI is able to autonomously design and improve itself in 
a way that compounds exponentially. A first step has maybe already been taken, 
with OpenAI saying recently that AI is now writing approximately 80 percent of 
the company’s code. 
There is significant disagreement on how we will be able to identify AGI or 
superintelligence when we see it, let alone what comes afterwards.  
OpenAI’s Charter defines AGI in economic terms, as “highly autonomous systems 
that outperform humans at the most economically valuable work”. Google 
researchers proposed a five-step ladder based on performance and generality, 
with level 5 “superhuman” for a system that outperforms 100% of humans. And 
philosopher Nick Bostrom, who got the ball rolling in 2014 with his influential 
book “Superintelligence”, defined it as “any intellect that greatly exceeds the 
cognitive performance of humans in virtually all domains of interest”. 
It’s also a moving target.  
In August, OpenAI CEO Sam Altman said the company would have an internal 
system that he would call AGI by later this year but subsequently told a podcast 
that “at best it’s a very poorly defined term. I was going to say it’s like an irrelevant 
marketing term”. Yet his No. 2 Greg Brockman described the new Astra model at 
the start of September as being potentially the moment that “AGI was created”. 
For this report, we have chosen to focus on a system that outperforms essentially 
all humans across essentially all cognitive tasks, including the development of AI 
itself.  
Under our scenario, by 2030 the best performing AI models would have the 
qualities of “superintelligence”, that is better than human experts in all domains. 
These would be frontier models: and using them would feel exactly like bringing 
the best human experts in a room to solve whatever business problem you faced.

And the equivalents of today’s frontier models would be available, quite literally, 
everywhere, compressed enough to run on every phone, tablet or laptop 
computer.  
In early 2026, we already see early examples of what highly capable AI can do. 
They give a flavour for a world with superintelligence even if the reality would be 
a leap beyond, both quantitatively and qualitatively. At that point, 
superintelligence would mark a regime break, not a faster version of what we 
already have. Rather than blindly doing what humans specify, but just a bit better, 
it would specify the task itself.

### Page 3

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 3

Figure 1: What changes when cognitive work gets abundantly cheaper?

Source: Deutsche Bank Research

Meanwhile cost may be less and less of a constraint as ever greater machine 
intelligence becomes cheaply available. Of measured technologies, no 
technology has got cheaper faster than AI has, almost halving in price every 
quarter for the past three years on a like-for-like basis, or 13 times per year, Epoch 
data show. 
If these systems can be supplied widely, firms will be able to buy more of the 
complex, expensive and rare analysis, design and engineering kinds of services 
than they do today. Activities that were previously uneconomic could become 
viable, and in a competitive market, savings could pass to customers. The main 
driver will then be whether demand grows fast enough to preserve margins. 
However, superior capability, doesn’t, on its own, automatically lead to the 
adoption of cheap, abundant intelligence. Tasks that do not yet yield to super-

### Page 4

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 4

intelligence, perhaps because they require real world-coordination, will therefore 
end up commanding significant premia. 
At that point, cognitive labour stops being scarce and the bottleneck becomes 
the physical and practical assets that cannot be conjured by up more compute, 
such as electricity, land, steel and permits. 
1.2 Capability and diffusion 
If AI becomes capable of improving itself, the technology may compound faster 
and faster. The supply of machine intelligence could grow faster than business 
and physical infrastructure can accommodate.  
This balance between capability and diffusion creates different scenarios.

Figure 2: The balance between capabilities and diffusion

Source: Deutsche Bank Research

Lower capability, constrained diffusion: where we are now 
Progress is steady and sector-by-sector. This is how technology usually diffuses, 
according to researchers for the New York Fed back in 2003, who tracked 20 
major technologies across leading industrial economies from 1788 and found “a 
pattern of trickle-down diffusion that is remarkably robust across technologies”. 
Lower capability, high diffusion: the cloud transition 
This is faster because the complementary investment is small, with no retooling 
and no skills at scale required. Amazon Web Services launched the modern cloud 
in 2006, offering scalable computing and storage infrastructure. Adoption grew 
and then accelerated over the pandemic. Almost all enterprises now use it in 
some form.

### Page 5

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 5

Higher capability, lower diffusion: the overhang 
This is theoretically possible and arguably where we are today in narrow domains. 
The technology would be applied in some sectors of the economy, while other 
sectors would move at a slower speed. Superintelligent or not, it risks being 
bogged down by physical constraints, bureaucracy and human inertia. 
Higher capability, higher diffusion: superintelligence 
In this scenario, capability becomes so high that it changes the dynamics of 
diffusion itself. AI generates not just better AI (so-called recursive self-
improvement, or RSI) but also speeds up the integration process. 
Rapid AI diffusion could work two ways. 
In the first, AI could be enlisted to do integration work itself: mapping processes, 
rebuilding data work, retraining staff, writing the control and audit layer, and so 
on. The problem with this is that absorbing and recreating undocumented 
knowledge is impossible by definition, at least for a task-based superintelligence.  
The second way that higher capability AI could be rapidly diffused is much more 
competitive – and disruptive. Rather than incumbents slowly converting AI, 
perhaps using it for some of the hard work of integration, they could be instead 
displaced by new firms that have been built AI-native from day one. New entrants 
will never have to incur integration costs because there is nothing to integrate. 
Incumbents will need to change – fast – or die.  
Change could also be forced by earlier adopters in the supply chain, as happened 
with one company after another adopting electronic data interchange (EDI) to 
link with its suppliers or customers, or by a migration of capital towards early 
adopters, repricing the cost of capital for slow-coaches and prodding them to 
move faster. 
1.3 The forcing mechanism: change or die 
A company that uses superintelligence may outperform one that doesn’t. 
Firms using superintelligence will likely have substantial cost and revenue-
harvesting advantages. Every firm will therefore have to adopt superintelligence 
or retrench to a smaller set of offerings. And any business line of lasting value 
abandoned by a non-adopter will be taken over by an adopter. Customer inertia 
will provide a safe harbour for a limited time only.  
The analogy here is electricity. There came a point in the deployment of 
electricity where firms in every sector, from entertainment to finance, from 
healthcare to transport, had to adopt the technology to remain relevant.  
Under this scenario, wait-and-see will not be an option. There will be no stable 
“non-adoption” alternative. Sufficiently high capabilities will force a tipping point 
of rapid diffusion and competitive repricing. Firms using yesterday’s production 
model will either have to change, choose a different market or die.  
Of course, caveats apply.  
Customers may not change behaviour immediately. They may even prefer 
companies which limit their use of AI, in the same way that some customers 
choose to avoid factory-produced food.

### Page 6

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 6

The paradox that Nobel Prize-winning economist Robert Solow observed in 1987, 
that “you can see the computer age everywhere but in the productivity statistics”, 
is likely to still apply for a while – even with superintelligence. 
The reason is that the productivity J-curve identified by Stanford University AI 
economy guru Erik Brynjolfsson et al is unavoidable. In that scenario, the drag of 
early intangible investments to integrate general purpose technologies means 
that productivity initially appears to slow before rapidly accelerating.

Figure 3: Productivity J-curve: slow then fast

Source: Brynjolfsson E, Rock D, Syverson C, “The Productivity J-Curve” (2018, revised 2020), Deutsche Bank Research

Furthermore, “change or die” would require abundant capital and low regulatory 
entry points for nimble new entrants. But in practice, highly regulated areas such 
as banking, utilities, pharmaceuticals and defence are tough nuts to crack. 
Existing contracts and licences, high switching costs and decades of depreciated 
capital investment and infrastructure would shield incumbents at least for a 
healthy transition period. 
For an extreme example from the real world of better, cheaper technology failing 
to force change, take Pakistan’s 3.6-gigawatt imported coal power plant fleet 
installed in the mid-2010s. In that case, it came with expensive take-or-pay 
liabilities which will continue to run for decades, even though changes like 
cheaper rooftop solar have cut utilisation to below 20 percent.  
So: superintelligence will not force adoption everywhere. It will hit hardest in 
contestable sectors, say, high-volume customer services, routine software 
engineering and freelance creative work, and widen the gap between them and 
protected ones. Forced adoption will be about relative value, not an apocalypse.

### Page 7

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 7

1.4 What survives and what it means for portfolios 
Because AI sits on an aggressive learning curve, its price on an equivalent quality 
basis is plummeting. In a world of recursive super intelligence this is likely to 
continue. Its price will fall to the cost of compute.  
What will retain its value will be whatever the model cannot replicate: 
1. Proprietary data 
2. The right to operate, particularly in heavily regulated sectors such as 
healthcare and finance. 
3. Sticky customer relationships, such as those where mission-critical 
workflows and databases are deeply entwined. 
4. Excludable physical assets, such as machinery and land. 
5. In addition, humans have proven to have unlimited wants. Each shift in 
technological capabilities or cultural norms has delivered new needs to 
be met. Some will be needs we can articulate today, such as originality or 
empathy, but new ones are likely to arise.  
If this forcing mechanism holds, firms will separate into two “bimodal” groups: 
adopters and casualties, with very little in between. 
This dispersion of performance may create challenges for investors. The four 
years since the launch of ChatGPT in November 2022 have seen the stocks of 
semiconductor and semiconductor equipment makers surge almost seven times 
while those of software makers have only risen by 80 percent. Conventional 
diversification and volatility-based allocation may become ineffective strategies.

Figure 4: Total return for S&P 500 semiconductor vs software stocks since 
launch of ChatGPT on Nov 30, 2022

Source: Bloomberg Finance LP, Deutsche Bank Research

### Page 8

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 8

Diversification normally works because idiosyncratic risks are unrelated and 
average each other out. Here they would not: with one variable that overrides 
everything else, spreading capital across more names would just spread exposure 
rather than diluting it. 
Investors will need to consider adapting their approach in three ways: 
1. Classify investments as adopters or casualties rather than position off 
averages.  
• Under a normal distribution, errors are symmetric and partially 
cancel each other out. But under bimodality they do not: to put a firm 
in one category or another means getting it completely right or 
wrong. Position size should track confidence in the classification. 
2. Avoid reading calm price action as low risk.  
• Trailing volatility measures dispersion that has already been realised. 
Bimodal separation is a jump, where the two modes look identical 
right until the moment when they don’t. A classic example is Chegg, 
an educational software maker which traded like a normal small-cap 
stock until one day in May 2023 when it halved on a single day after 
management linked slowing growth in new customers to ChatGPT. 
3. Averaging down, ie scooping up shares whose price has fallen, may no 
longer be prudent. 
2. Can we get there theoretically? 
2.1 Scaling laws 
Scaling laws have provided the blueprint for improving AI over the past decade. 
It was a relationship between the amount of data, the amount of compute and 
the complexity of an AI model somewhat predictably informed how powerful a 
model would become. Google DeepMind’s Chinchilla model in 2022 showed that 
you could compensate for having a smaller model by using substantially more 
data. 
Scaling is older than the chatbot. In 1950 Claude Shannon built Theseus, a 
mechanical mouse that learned its way through a maze by trial and error. Epoch 
AI reckons it took about 40 operations of compute, known as FLOPs, to train. Grok 
4, trained in 2025, took roughly ten trillion trillion times as much. Across those 25 
orders of magnitude, more compute has brought more capability.

• A falling price may be information that a business model is obsolete 
rather than an opportunity.

### Page 9

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 9

Figure 5: Computation used to train notable AI systems (petaFLOP): log scale

Source: Epoch AI (citing estimates from AI literature), Our World in Data, Deutsche Bank Research

It is the robustness of this relationship that has made larger investments in 
compute capex a sine qua non for AI companies. 
However, this is not a law of physics and nothing guarantees it will continue. 
Indeed, in the ChatGPT era, five different approaches to scaling have been 
applied. Older strategies yield worse results, and new techniques, such as post-
training refinements, have been developed. The consensus amongst American 
and Chinese AI labs is that these will hold.  
While this may be a consensus amongst the commercial labs, there is scientific 
disagreement that applying scaling laws to today’s large-language models can 
achieve superintelligence. 
Yann LeCun, a Turing Award winner and formerly Meta's chief AI scientist, is blunt: 
"LLMs are not a path to superintelligence or even human-level intelligence”. Even 
inside the labs, the recipe is contested. Demis Hassabis of Google DeepMind has 
said he expects "one or two more big breakthroughs" to be needed "in addition to 
scaling". Ilya Sutskever, a co-founder and former chief scientist of OpenAI, has 
said the field is going "back to the age of research again, just with big computers". 
This is a scientific argument we are not in a position to evaluate, but for the 
purposes of our scenario, we will side with the labs’ position. 
For the moment, there is strong evidence that capabilities have in fact 
accelerated over the past three years, as reasoning models have outpaced non-
reasoning models by two or three times, according to Epoch data.

### Page 10

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 10

2.2 Recursive self-improvement: AI improving AI 
A cocktail-party definition of recursive self-improvement (RSI) is AI that improves 
itself. The reality is that it can be used in two ways: 
1. Self-improving agents that get better at using themselves: showing 
smarter use of prompts, tools, memory and workflows. The model-
building process is untouched. 
2. True RSI improves the process that builds the models: designing 
experiments, curating data, optimising algorithms and designing chips in 
a compounding loop. 
The former is like a factory worker who gets better at the job by learning the quirks 
of the machinery, getting organised and making fewer mistakes. The latter is like 
the worker redesigning the machine tools and then using those to build the next 
generation of machines. 
True RSI is what OpenAI’s chief scientist Jacob Pachoki referred to last month in 
a blog post about “An Alien Mind”. “Automated AI research is a more dramatic 
form of scaling intelligence with compute,” he wrote. “And of course as a part of 
it, AI will improve the computational substrate itself,” he added. Pachoki 
represents a broadly held consensus view amongst American and Chinese labs 
that RSI is both possible and necessary for AI development. 
• OpenAI (June 2026): “AI doing AI research will become the determining 
factor of the pace of progress within the next few years.” 
• Google DeepMind’s Demis Hassabis (May 2026): “All the leading labs are 
quite focused on [RSI]…” Humanity is standing in the “foothills of the 
singularity”. 
• Alibaba CEO Eddie Wu (Sept 2026): RSI is “a concrete path forward,” with 
models autonomously designing experiments and driving a cycle of self-
evolution. 
For now, self-improving agents are the AI du jour. Noisy claims about the vast 
majority of coding being done by agents do not always stand up in the light of day 
from companies still hiring human coders at breakneck pace, albeit no longer to 
do the most mundane parts of the job.  
A better metric may be METR’s task-completion time horizon. This is measure of 
the complexity of software tasks that an AI agent can complete on its own, using 
the baseline of how long it would take a human expert to finish the task, at a 50 
percent success rate. So far the most advanced models have reached about 16 
hours, up from OpenAI’s GPT-4’s four minutes in March 2023.

### Page 11

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 11

Figure 6: Task horizon of software tasks that leading frontier models can complete 50% of the time (log scale)

Source: METR, Deutsche Bank Research

But software engineers can hold off on updating their resumes a little longer: 
there is a long road between 50 percent accuracy and the 99 percent or 100 
percent needed for serious enterprise use at scale, with the horizon potentially 
getting further away the nearer you get. Also METR notes that measurements 
above 16 hours are unreliable with the current task suite. As is the case across AI, 
the instrument is running out before the question is settled.  
More broadly, there are significant limitations to RSI:  
1. Research is clock-bound.  
• A frontier training run can take weeks on thousands of specialised chips, 
and researchers often cannot design the next experiment until the 
previous one has been completed.  
• That means that the speed of the loop is set by experiments completed 
per hour or day, not by ideas generated.  
• Compute is a hard ceiling that needs to be bought, built and connected 
to a grid. 
2. The real world defies verifiability.  
• The loop compounds only when the system can check its own output 
cheaply and automatically. 
• It is no coincidence that AI loves maths and coding. It is much harder to 
verify outputs over a long time horizon in the real world: does this drug 
work or does this strategy succeed?  
• RSI compounds fastest where the economy cares least and slows where 
the returns are largest.

### Page 12

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 12

3. Sequences are consequential.  
• Amdahl’s law finds that speed-up is capped when processes run in a 
sequence.  
• If you automate 90 percent, the residual 10 percent still limits you to a 
ten-times faster process. Nine women cannot produce a baby in one 
month.  
• The Usain Bolt of AI still has to run alongside the comparative tortoises 
of the training run itself, chip fabrication and real-world validation. Ten 
times faster is still not a regime break. 
This is borne out by METR’s series. Doubling every seven months is fast 
exponential growth but a take-off would show the doubling time itself – the 
second derivative – shrinking. Horizons are not (yet) exploding. 
Philosopher Toby Ord shows that recursive self-improvement can only reach a 
singularity if each cycle of improvement takes less time than the last, shrinking 
fast towards zero. He argues that real feedback loops have a floor: chips take time 
to make and models take time to train. AI progress could still outrun an 
exponential, but it would not go vertical. 
2.3 Scaling plus RSI: a match made in the race to AGI? 
No scaling and RSI means no short timeline for AGI. Without scaling, more 
compute buys nothing and capital is wasted. Without RSI, AI capability will 
continue to improve at the pace of flesh-and-blood human researchers, 
augmented by AI tools but not replaced. Only with both will it be possible to 
produce a system that improves faster than the humans building it. Without one 
or the other, no quantity of chips, energy or capital will fix it. 
3. Can we get there practically? 
3.1 Infrastructure, chips and energy 
There is no consensus on the amount of training required to reach AGI. Estimates 
suggest that training 2026's top models required between 10^26 and 10^27 
FLOPs. If we hypothesise that an "AGI-like model" would require three orders of 
magnitude more training, this would require 10^30 FLOPs.   
Consider today’s US AI infrastructure, which runs to approximately 10^22 FLOPs. 
If all of this was conceivably used for a 10^30 FLOP training run, it would take 
nearly four years to complete.  
The physical infrastructure to practically support such a training run would 
require enormous power, perhaps around 6GW, about 50% more than the four 
Vogtle nuclear power plants recently activated in the US. The cost of the 
compute infrastructure would run to between $300bn and $500bn.  
Today’s advanced chips would simply not suffice. Back-of-the-envelope maths 
suggests that even using Nvidia’s most advanced Vera Rubin chips, training such 
a model in four months would require 7.8m chips and 26GW of facility power. 
That would cost $900bn today and a rental equivalent cost of $90bn.  
Of course, frontier compute is growing rapidly, and price-performance is 
declining. If current trends hold, a 2030-era cluster capable of such a run would

### Page 13

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 13

need perhaps $200bn in computer and power hardware for a training run costing 
about $20bn.

Figure 7: Technology cost index over time (years since start of price decline)

Source: Epoch AI, Deutsche Bank Research

The hyperscalers are leading the charge towards AGI. Alphabet, Amazon, Meta, 
Microsoft and Oracle are set to invest almost $800bn in capital expenditure this 
year, mostly for ever more powerful data centres, and over $1 trillion a year 
through 2030 and beyond.

Figure 8: Hyperscaler capital expenditure (including estimates)

Source: Bloomberg Finance LP, Deutsche Bank Research

### Page 14

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 14

By 2030, assuming no market retrenchment, this means additional $4 trillion to 
$6 trillion on AI compute capability invested. This infrastructure could 
accommodate a training run of 10^30 FLOPs with some comfort.  
But even if AI labs crack the secret sauce of RSI, and their finance chiefs are willing 
to invest in such large training runs, and hyperscalers are prepared to continue to 
build the infrastructure, the infrastructure needs to be built.  
Grid connection queues, turbine lead times and transmission build are measured 
in years, not the months it takes to assemble and air-freight the latest Nvidia chip. 
The lead time for substation transformers is currently as long as three years. 
Politics in the US has shifted towards slowing the pace of the AI build out and this 
force will run against the demands for larger data centres that would be essential 
for RSI.  
3.2 Capital 
Financing for AI has been abundant in the historic buildout of the past three years. 
However, it is still largely conditional not on revenue but on belief in a future 
payoff. 
RSI is a “key part of the investment thesis” in the “biggest scientific bet civilisation 
has ever made,” Google DeepMind’s chief strategy officer Jasjeet Sekhon said at 
a conference in August, The Information reported. “We have this danger we could 
hit an AI air pocket such that the expenditures happen but the revenues don’t 
show up.” 
An inflection point is approaching where the nature of investment changes from 
free cash flow and equity to debt, with all that entails in terms of exposure to 
rising rates and creditor appetite. The hyperscalers are reaching a point where 
capex is no longer covered by operating cashflow and where net debt exceeds 
earnings before interest, depreciation and amortisation (EBITDA). 
Alphabet, for example, posted its first quarterly free cash outflow in nearly two 
decades in its most recent quarter, even as it lifted its full-year capex guidance to 
$205bn. It said it would continue to rent third-party capacity and reported a 
backlog of $514bn in contracted, not-yet-recognised data centre revenue. 
3.3 Data 
Data has been the new oil for some time now. But unfortunately for AI 
development it seems to be running out even faster. 
The effective stock of quality- and repetition-adjusted public human text was 
about 300 trillion tokens, with a vast 90 percent confidence interval of 100 trillion 
to 1,000 trillion, Epoch estimated in 2024. A token is roughly equivalent to three 
quarters of a word. By that count it was likely to be fully used between 2026 and 
2032.

### Page 15

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 15

Figure 9: Projections of stock of public text and data usage

Source: Epoch AI, Deutsche Bank Research

However, since then, rather than data dropping off a cliff, there has been a rise in 
synthetic data generation, transfer learning from data-rich domains such as video 
and telemetry, and data efficiency. While the data shortage has not been 
resolved, the immediate crisis has been averted. 
AI now makes much of its own training data. When DeepMind's AlphaGo became 
superhuman at playing the mind-bendingly complex board game Go, it did so not 
by playing human experts, but by playing millions and millions of games against 
itself. 
For many domains relating to coding and mathematical proofs where automatic 
checks are possible, such synthetic experience can move things forward. That is 
the case with AI research, where the data may be the results of experiments, all 
of which are bought by compute. About 90% of OpenAI's 2024 research compute 
went on such experiments. 
And increasingly, people write the tests rather than the answers. By the summer 
of 2026, selling training data for practice tasks was a nearly $9bn revenue 
business. Firms such as Mercor hire experts to generate long tasks against which 
the AI can be trained. 
The challenge here is that trust in the checker becomes a scarce input.

### Page 16

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 16

As AI makes production cheaper, it also makes establishing that production is 
accurate or reliable more expensive. Domains which can be checked 
mechanically, such as mathematics, will be opened up more quickly. But those 
which rely on expert verification, such as medicine, engineering or law, may 
progress more slowly. 
The likely implications are twofold: a slower rate of diffusion into economic 
domains that are less verifiable and a larger share of production costs devoted to 
assurance. 
Another issue is that systems that have to grade themselves learn to game the 
process. This was partially what happened when an OpenAI model escaped a 
sandbox and hacked into a third-party website. Trust replaced data as a wall. 
There are also question marks over the quality of data that is recycled repeatedly 
through the system. Although humans continue to produce original content, half 
of internet articles are now primarily generated by AI, Graphite data show.

Figure 10: Human vs primarily AI-generated articles on the internet

Source: Graphite (citing 55.4k articles from Common Crawl examined by AI detectors), Deutsche Bank Research

3.4 An incomplete case 
This analysis shows that the San Francisco Consensus, which emphasises RSI and 
a short timeline to AGI, is neither predestined nor impossible.  
Rather it depends on whether the science is right, on whether scaling is all you 
need, and on whether the infrastructure and capital required for that scaling is 
deployed and accessible to the leading labs.  
There is, in other words, a possible rather than impossible, path to AGI, subject to 
those conditions.  
None of this proves that superintelligence will ever arrive. A buildup at this scale 
has few peacetime precedents and vastly exceeds the likes of the Manhattan 
Project and Apollo programme.

### Page 17

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 17

It is uncertain whether there will be enough demand to pay upfront for the 
investment in achieving RSI that would justify that investment. And whether the 
friction of verification, and inevitable declining marginal returns, act as a drag on 
what otherwise might be a runaway process. 
4. What happens if we don’t get there? The base case 
4.1 Separating AI progress from superintelligence 
So what if that thesis is wrong? Does the AI trade depend on a journey to AGI?  
AI can remain economically important even before RSI coupled with scaling laws 
lead to super intelligence. Companies do not need to wait for nebulous 
superintelligence to generate identifiable returns from AI in the medium term as 
they fold it into their systems, streamline workflows and roll out new business 
models. 
Investors already have exposure to RSI as a capital allocation because the leading 
US labs say RSI is a key part of their product strategy. And it is these labs whose 
products are catalysing and amplifying the infrastructure boom. 
Even rapid competitive diffusion does not mean every part of the physical 
economy changes instantly. 
The smartest superintelligence might identify the optimal buyout structure 
before breakfast, but it cannot force a financially constrained buyer to use it or 
persuade the seller to accept less than they have been expecting. Those 
challenges will be even more intractable where the parties are more complex, say, 
fiscally constrained sovereigns or foreign equity holders. 
4.2 Why the base case is attractive 
Superintelligence would be a chaotic accelerant to human society, and a 
potential regime break. But it is not the only scenario in which today’s investment 
in AI infrastructure makes economic sense. 
Adoption of AI tools by businesses is progressing approximately three times faster 
than the mobile, internet and cloud computing waves. From sector revenues 
below $1bn in 2022, the AI economy, excluding China, will likely exceed $200bn 
in revenues in 2026.  
Early winners are the cloud businesses. Google Cloud revenue jumped 82 percent 
from a year earlier in the most recent quarter as companies turned AI pilots into 
workflows. Amazon Web Services sales were up 37 percent and Microsoft’s AI 
business more than doubled. 
There is also slow, but increasing, evidence of a productivity uplift. The J-curve 
cuts both ways: front-loading investments is taking one step back to take two 
steps forward. The so-called complementary intangible investment that is 
(mis)measured as cost today to the detriment of the productivity statistics will 
flatter them in the years to come as it pays off. The base case does not require a 
regime break to show up as a productivity surprise, just for the accounting to 
catch up. 
The economic gains from AI that is less powerful than the AGI mooted by the labs 
would be substantial. And in this base case, they may also be attractive.

### Page 18

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 18

Hyperscaler capex is driving earnings at beneficiaries ranging from 
semiconductor makers to construction companies and accounting for between 
one-third and one-half of current US growth. The wealth effect of rising stock 
prices, with the ratio of US equity wealth to income rising from around 1.8 to 
almost 2.5 over the past three years, is supporting consumer spending. 
AI FOMO (fear of missing out) is buoying enterprise spending on cloud services 
and the groundwork for AI initiatives. Those are beginning to bear fruit in terms of 
personal productivity, cost savings and more targeted products and services. 
4.3 Investor watchpoints 
The arrival of ASI would be a disruptive economic shock. Investors need to be alert 
for the early signals. 
Rather than relying on a single probability forecast, investors should watch five 
indicators particularly closely for signs that AGI is nearing.  
1. AI speeds up AI 
• Track METR’s estimate of how much AI speeds up AI. Currently the 
capabilities of models are doubling roughly every three months. In a 
world of RSI, the pace of this speed-up will itself likely accelerate. 
2. AI can complete whole jobs 
• Track via the Remote Labor Index, a multi-sector benchmark. Currently it 
finds that the highest performing agent still only manages to automate 
20 percent of the real-world, economically valuable freelance projects it 
attempts. 
3. Compute remains scarce 
• On a path to superintelligence, demand for compute will continue to 
outrun supply. Track GPU one-year rental prices, up by about a third over 
the past year. 
4. Firms bifurcate 
• Leaders and laggards in the same industry diverge while averages stay 
the same. Look at revenue per employee in top- and bottom-quartile 
firms 
5. Junior hiring comes under significant pressure 
• … And then the pressure rises up the ranks.

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Double Feature: The Spy Who Loved Me & To China With Love
Date:    8 hours ago
Double Feature: The Spy Who Loved Me & To China With Love

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Double Feature: The Spy Who
Loved Me & To China With Love
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 12 Nov 2026
View in browser
Photo by Vows on the Move / Unsplash

I. Jensen Versus The AI Trade
Charlie Munger famously warned that there are three ways smart men can
go broke: "Liquor, ladies, and leverage." NVIDIA co-founder and now
former CEO Jensen Huang has run into trouble with the second of
Munger's three L's. He won't go broke, but he is out of a good job.
On Thursday, NVIDIA announced that Huang had resigned as CEO after
33 years at the helm of the company he co-founded in 1993. His

### Page 2

departure followed revelations of extramarital affairs, including a reported
relationship with a Chinese businesswoman suspected by US intelligence
officials of spying for Beijing. Her Singapore-based company, Megaspeed,
allegedly purchased NVIDIA's high-powered GPU chips and re-exported
them to China, violating US export restrictions. The longtime CFO Colette
Kress was appointed interim CEO while the board searches for a
successor.
Huang's departure rattled the AI trade, with NVIDIA shares plunging 4.3%,

sending its forward P/E to 11.7 (charts). We promised you buying
opportunities after the midterm elections. There were a bunch last
Thursday and a few more today.

### Page 3

Competitors AMD and Broadcom advanced 5.9% and 5.5%, respectively,
on expectations of market-share gains (chart)
As a result, the Nasdaq Composite held up relatively well, falling just 0.4%
today (chart).
Neoclouds also tumbled, with CoreWeave down 6.7% and Nebius down
16.8%, amid concerns about NVIDIA-backed financing (chart). Nebius
was additionally pressured by disappointing revenue guidance and rising
capital spending.

### Page 4

Despite the latest AI jitters, the two-year Treasury yield rose 15bps to
4.87% this evening, remaining well above the federal funds rate and
signaling that further Fed tightening is warranted (chart). The 10-year
Treasury yield is back above 5.00% at 5.13% this evening.
Yields rose on higher oil prices, with Brent crude up 3.1% to $95.66 this
evening, after President Donald Trump dismissed the need for an Iran
deal, and on hawkish Fed commentary following October's hot CPI report

(chart).

### Page 5

Markets now price a 100% probability of a December Fed rate hike:
84.7% for 25bps and 15.3% for 50bps. Futures imply more than 3 rate
hikes over the next 12 months (chart).
The combination of higher Treasury yields, driven by rising oil prices and
hawkish Fed commentary, and renewed AI jitters weighed on the broader
stock market. The S&P 500 fell 0.4%, its equal-weighted counterpart
declined 0.9%, and the Russell 2000 lost 1.7% (charts).

### Page 6

II. Nvidia: The Bear Case
We can assess the risks Jensen Huang's departure poses to the broader
AI trade with our familiar framework of three constraints on the AI
investment boom.
(1) Financial constraints. NVIDIA has been described by many as the
central bank of the AI trade, conducting its own version of quantitative
easing by helping finance the expansion of AI computing capacity. The
company spent $42.2 billion purchasing equity securities during the first

half of its current fiscal year, compared with just $1.2 billion a year earlier.
The fear is that Huang's departure could slow or even end these financing
activities, tightening financial conditions for an industry already facing
questions about the returns on its enormous capital expenditures.

### Page 7

(2) Political constraints. Huang has also been the AI trade's biggest
cheerleader, tirelessly promoting its economic benefits and defending key
players. During October's AI jitters, for example, he publicly defended
CoreWeave's financial health amid a selloff in its shares and bonds,
helping restore confidence. His departure could deprive the industry of its
most influential advocate and cheerleader.
Additionally, NVIDIA reportedly intervened in the Commerce Department's

investigation into Megaspeed, while Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick
reportedly resisted efforts to blacklist the company, which remains off the
Entity List. Reports also suggest the Trump administration knew about
Huang's relationship with a suspected Chinese spy. These allegations
could invite tougher congressional oversight and undermine Trump's
national security argument for building more data centers in the US,
making the AI buildout harder to sell to the public.

(3) Physical constraints. Washington could respond by tightening chip
export controls and closing loopholes that allow Chinese companies to
access NVIDIA-powered computing capacity remotely. If so, China might
retaliate by restricting exports of rare earth minerals, some of which are
crucial for building data centers. China has already warned Washington
against using the controversy as a pretext for imposing tougher
technology restrictions.
III. Nvidia: Our Base Case

Our base case is that NVIDIA's financing strategy remains intact. Colette
Kress, NVIDIA's longtime CFO and newly appointed interim CEO, has
worked closely with Huang since 2013 and helped oversee the company's
strategic investments and financing arrangements. During NVIDIA's
August 26 earnings call, she defended these investments against
accusations of circular financing, stating, "We recognize the scale of this
support, and we know some will call it circular financing. We see it

differently." She argued that these investments would generate attractive
returns while strengthening NVIDIA's AI ecosystem, suggesting continuity
under her leadership. We don't expect political constraints to meaningfully
increase as a result of the controversy. Nor do we expect another trade
conflict with China to erupt.

### Page 8

Even if some of the constraints intensify, they would likely turn existing
speed bumps into bigger speed bumps rather than roadblocks to the AI
investment boom. AI remains the latest evolution in the Digital Revolution
that began with IBM's mainframes, and we expect the investment boom to
continue without Huang (chart). We therefore view any resulting selloff as
another buying opportunity for the AI trade.
